# Build Progress Tracker

Update this as you build. Mark items `[x]` done, `[~]` in progress, `[!]` blocked. Add a note for any deviation from the design and why. This doubles as the **post-build verification report** required by `IMPLEMENTATION_BRIEF.md`.

Legend: `[ ]` not started · `[~]` in progress · `[x]` done & verified · `[!]` blocked/needs decision

---

## 📍 Current Status & Session Handoff — READ FIRST

**Branch:** always cut a fresh one from `main` — this file no longer names one, see below. **Deploy:** Railway, Root Directory `design_handoff_modern_gentlemen/starter` — **live at https://modern-gentlemen-website-production.up.railway.app** (this file went eight phases without recording the URL anywhere, which meant nobody could check a deploy without opening the Railway dashboard). **Database:** Supabase project `qnfoztnyxhubnnulpfwt` — schema applied and seeded, **live**. Every migration in `supabase/migrations/` is applied (⚠️ **this line no longer names a range — it has been wrong three times**, reading `0015` while sixteen and then seventeen existed, and `0022` while twenty-six did. The `SessionStart` hook prints the true count and the latest filename; prefer it over any sentence here). Apply new ones through the Supabase MCP: the GitHub integration is connected but **cannot safely run this repo's migrations** (version-scheme mismatch — see Known issues).

> ⚠️ **Branch guidance — read this before starting work.** Always start from `main`:
> `git fetch origin main && git checkout -b <new-branch> origin/main`
>
> **This file used to name the current branch here, and that line went stale five times.** The pattern was always identical: the branch merges, the correction is deferred until afterwards, and afterwards the only place to write it is a branch nobody should commit to. Patching the sentence again was tried — the last version even retired itself on merge — and it *still* left "Phase 6a is on `<branch>`" in the present tense one commit later.
>
> So the name is gone. A current-branch name is perishable by construction, and this file cannot hold perishable facts safely. **The `SessionStart` hook already prints the live branch every session** (`node design_handoff_modern_gentlemen/starter/scripts/status.mjs`), which is a source that cannot go stale because it is computed, not written.
>
> **Do not build on any `claude/*` branch on the remote.** Every one of them is either merged or abandoned: `-ntslwo` (`d1dc9d3`), `-ngmzu7` (`0e83650`), `claude/project-status-review-nz8w9i`, `claude/progress-migration-versioning`, `claude/docs-refresh-after-phase5`, `claude/project-status-review-8la85a` (Phase 6a, PR #11), `claude/phase-7a-public-rewire` (Phase 7a, PR #12), `claude/env-credential-validation` (PR #15) `claude/phase-7b-store-from-db` (Phase 7b) `claude/session-setup-review-9ihb9i` (XML ingestion, PR #26, `485912d`) and `claude/session-setup-review-g564ri` (the Shopify adapter, PR #28, `6ed0447`; then reused for password recovery + the proxy redirect fix, PR #29, `74fbd5d`) and `claude/session-setup-review-gpwraf` (drag-from-library, PR #32, `9d87568`) and `claude/session-setup-review-wesizw` (templates' named areas, PR #52, `cbe7937`) and `claude/session-setup-review-wleu2z` (Track A Phase 7 — the image optimiser + performance budget, PR #64, `a6ccd26`; then reused for the docs cleanup, PR #65, `871b505`) and `claude/session-setup-review-sw5u79` (the brand-decision + contrast work and the newsletter capture, PR #69, `40fb9e2`; then reused for the four hardening slices, PR #70, `11ed88d`, and again for the cold-start handoff, PR #71, `17f455c`) and `claude/session-setup-review-cq00g8` (the RLS suite + `0018`, PR #37, `02fd7fb`; then reused three times more — the docs-only PR #38, `0019` + the patterns admin (PR #39, `ba3ecb7`), and `0020` (PR #40, `6c259f3`)). This list is history and only ever grows; it needs no edit to stay true.
>
> ✅ **`rls.test.ts` no longer carries a `KNOWN GAP` test, and this note records what the pattern was for rather than warning about a live one.** Both characterisation tests it once held have now completed the full cycle: asserted as the wrong-but-current behaviour, cited to justify a migration, then **inverted in the same commit as the fix** — `is_system` for `0018`, `draft_data` for `0020`. The technique is the reusable part. A gap recorded only in prose is a gap nobody notices closing; asserted, the day someone fixes it the suite goes red and says so. **If you add another, name it `KNOWN GAP` and write the instruction to invert it inside the test**, because that is what made both of them survive the months between discovery and fix.
>
> ✅ **Nothing is in flight.** The last ten in-flight bullets — Shopify (PR #28), drag-from-library (PR #32, merged as `9d87568`), the drag-reordering fixes (PR #50, merged as `e9b8225`), templates' named areas (PR #52, merged as `cbe7937`), synced patterns (PR #54, merged as `3d5511a`), previewing a template area (PR #59, merged as `20beee4`), the three-slice branch (PR #61, merged as `8b86fa1`), the scheduling branch (PR #62, merged as `0a7d530`), the Phase 7 branch (PR #64, merged as `a6ccd26`) the variant-picker branch (PR #67, merged as `6f90a34`) and the cold-start handoff (PR #71, merged as `17f455c`) — were each **deleted in the session that opened them, minutes after the merge**. Eleven in a row is a habit rather than an accident, and the mechanism is worth recording as the fix: each bullet was written with its own deletion condition inside it, and each deletion happened while the session that owned it was still running. The five failures before that shared one shape — the correction was left until "afterwards", and afterwards the only place to write it was a branch nobody should commit to.
>
> ⚠️ **One exception, or the rule never terminates: a PR whose only content is deleting an in-flight bullet does not get a bullet of its own.** Found by walking into it — PR #35 was docs-only, its bullet had to be removed after it merged, and removing it needs another PR, which by the letter of the rule would need another bullet. The purpose of a bullet is to stop a new session branching into a change it does not know about; a PR that deletes one sentence about a merged PR cannot surprise anybody.
>
> **The steady-state sentence above is false whenever a PR is in flight**, which is exactly when a new session is most likely to read it and branch anyway. The ingestion phase hit this and handled it by adding a bullet here naming the open PR, marked for deletion on merge — and then deleting it in the same session the merge happened, which is the half that has failed five times before. **If you leave a PR open at the end of a session, add that bullet back**; a branch carrying an interface the next phase depends on is the case that actually costs something.

> 🟢 **Where the project actually stands, for a session starting cold.** Both
> tracks are code-complete, and the engineering backlog is empty of anything a
> session may start on its own initiative. The live Known-issues list holds
> **eleven** genuinely open items, and they split three ways:
>
> - **Five are accepted, recorded so nobody "fixes" them.** dnd-kit's 50ms
>   post-drop click window (upstream, and the guard is worth more than the window
>   costs), the unpublishable empty `columns` row (its slot declares `min: 1` on
>   purpose), the hover preview's mount cost, `visibility.devices` being stored
>   and deliberately never applied at render, and the fresh-container credential
>   requirement. Read the bullet before touching any of them.
> - **Two are environmental** — the E2E resource-starvation mode, and Supabase's
>   Redirect URLs allow list, which is deployment configuration no test, build or
>   `check-env.mjs` run can assert.
> - **Four are real engineering, none urgent**: the `draft_data` column a
>   role-less authenticated account can still read (the largest — a
>   `security definer` read path across seven document tables, 41 read sites, and
>   it changes how every admin screen reads); an ingestion run holding a server
>   action open for its duration; dropping a block into a *non-empty* container;
>   and `resolve_preview`'s missing rate limit, **which just got cheap** — `0026`
>   ships `rate_limit_hit()` and `consumeRateLimit` is one call, so the only open
>   question is what identity a preview link keys on.
>
> Everything else that gates launch is a **brand decision**, not code: see
> "Decisions the brand owns" and "Not code at all" below. **The next session's
> first job is to ask which, not to pick one.**
>

> The same paragraph also named two ingestion gaps — no image import, manual runs

> them.**
>

> mistake in the session that wrote this paragraph** — a stale local `main`
> pointer eight PRs behind listed four closed items as open, and the answer was
> most of the way written before the branch was re-synced. `git fetch` before you
> read this file's status claims, and prefer the `SessionStart` hook to any
> sentence in it.

Two tracks now exist. **Track A (front-end)** is complete and pixel-verified. **Track B (backend + admin platform)** is in progress: the data foundation, auth, the block system, publishing, the admin builder, media, the CMS, the products admin, navigation, the theme editor, **ingestion behind both of its adapters** and **the whole public read path** are done. ⚠️ This sentence ended "ingestion is not" for three phases after ingestion started and one after it shipped — **Phase 6b is now complete in full**. `pattern` support is done, and **`template` support is done too**: every one of the six document types now opens in the builder. What is left in Track B is the Phase 1 leftovers and the follow-ups in the next-phase list, none of which is a missing builder capability.

Live state (branch, commits, migrations, test counts) is printed automatically at session start by a hook. Run it any time:
`node design_handoff_modern_gentlemen/starter/scripts/status.mjs`

| B | RLS — delete permissions | `0022`: six `x.delete` permissions become real gates — one RESTRICTIVE delete policy per table | ✅ **done & verified end to end — CI green on PR #56** (`50dfcab`), all three jobs: `rls.test.ts` 32 → **42 tests**, integration 104 → **122**. **`0022` applied to the live project and replayed idempotently.** The defect: a `for all` write policy covers DELETE and permissive policies are OR-ed, so `x.write OR x.delete` made every delete permission *additive* — it could grant and never refuse. | B | Store — the PDP's two price defects | `memberUnitPrice` (`lib/domain/pricing.ts`), `offersFor`/`variantOffer` (`lib/domain/seo.ts`), `product_variants` in `getPublishedProductSeo` | ✅ done (code). **No migration — `0005` built `product_variants` and `0019` already scoped its reads to a published parent; both were already relied on by the picker and were re-checked here rather than assumed.** The two things the variant picker made visible and did not fix. **The member price was computed in pounds** — `Math.round(145 * 0.85)` quoted £123 where the bag charges £123.25, so the page and the checkout disagreed by 25p about the site's own headline benefit, in violation of the integer-pence rule. **And `productJsonLd` advertised the product's own `price_pence` as a lone `Offer`**, so a PDP showing £159.99 for the large told a crawler £145 — the mismatch Merchant Center suspends a feed over. 1292 unit tests (+15), build green with every rendering mode unchanged (`/` `○`, `/[category]` and `/product/[slug]` `●` with 16 slugs), **16/16 baselines**, 28/28 a11y, 14/14 perf |

Ink and fill want opposite luminances, so they are two tokens now. 1294 unit tests, build green with every rendering mode unchanged, **16/16 baselines**, 28/28 a11y with contrast enforced everywhere bar three named pairs, 14/14 perf |

The `KNOWN GAP` test inverted in the same commit — the **third** to complete that cycle after `0018` and `0020` |
| A | Phase 7 — contrast reaches ZERO | `--mg-*-rgb` twins for `bg`/`fg`/`surface`/`bd`, the `/60` opacity floor, the light grey ramp, `ACCEPTED_CONTRAST_GAPS` deleted | ✅ done (code). **No migration.** **Every WCAG A/AA rule now passes on all 11 public routes in both themes, contrast included, with no exceptions list and no disabled rules.** The path: 204 → 175 → 34 → 6 → **110** → 0. ⚠️ **The 110 is the finding.** ~413 `text-mg-fg/NN` classes had compiled to nothing since Track A, so muted text painted at full strength and *passed contrast by accident*; repairing the utilities took the count 6 → 110 because the bug had been hiding every one of those failures. `--mg-fg` needs alpha **0.59** to clear 4.5 on `#f4f4f4`, so 177 call sites below `/60` were raised. ⚠️ **The light ramp did NOT have to collapse** — three separate notes in this repo said it must; anything ≤ `#707070` passes both light grounds, so `#5a5a5a`/`#707070` keeps both steps. 1301 unit tests, build green with every rendering mode unchanged, **16/16 baselines re-recorded** (all sixteen genuinely moved, two by a pixel of height), 27/27 a11y, 14/14 perf |
| B | Store — the "From" price on `/shop` | `cardPricePence` (`lib/domain/variants.ts`) + `ProductCard` | ✅ done (code). **No migration.** The third and last of the defects the variant picker exposed. A card read £145 for a product whose only in-stock size costs £159.99. It quotes the **lowest sellable** variant now — the same rule `productJsonLd`'s `AggregateOffer` uses, asserted against it in a test so the card and the structured data behind it cannot disagree — and says "From" only when the buyable sizes actually differ, so three sizes at one price still read "£145" |
| B | Public — the newsletter actually captures | `lib/domain/newsletter.ts`, `0024`, the repository/service, `POST /api/newsletter`, `useNewsletterSignup`, both bands rewired | ✅ done (code). **`0024` applied to the live project and probed five ways.** ⚠️ **The backlog said "the newsletter captures to Supabase and reaches no ESP" and that was false**: no table, no route, no repository, no service. Both bands discarded the address — `CtaBand`'s input was not even controlled — and then claimed success on **seven live pages**. **The first table `anon` may write to**, so the grants are the gate: INSERT on three columns, no SELECT at all, probed to confirm a visitor cannot read the list, cannot forge `status='confirmed'`, and that a role-less signed-in account cannot read it either. ⚠️ **Three client-call shapes failed before one worked** — `.select()`, then `.upsert()`, both needing a SELECT `anon` does not have; the answer is a plain insert that treats `23505` as success, which makes the non-disclosure a fact rather than a promise. 1323 unit tests, 16/16 baselines, 27/27 a11y, 14/14 perf |
| B | Hardening — `0025` and the launch-gate audit | `site_settings` read scoped to staff, `replace_feed_mappings()` (transactional, `security invoker`), the checkout's false confirmation line | ✅ done (code). **`0025` applied to the live project and probed five ways.** Two unrelated defects from one audit pass. **`site_settings` was `for select using (true)`** — world-readable since `0001`, empty, and read by nothing (checked, not assumed), so closing it cost nothing and fails safe. **`replaceMappings` was a delete-then-insert across two round trips**: an interrupted save left a source with zero mappings and no error, which had been surfacing as E2E flakiness. Now one `plpgsql` function, so both statements roll back together — **proven by making the insert half fail and confirming the previous set survives**, where the old path left 0. ⚠️ `security invoker`, not `definer`: a definer function would bypass the editor's RLS and break this repo's oldest standing rule |
| B | Admin — patterns' description and category | `listPatternCategories`/`setPatternDetails`, the create + edit dialogs, and category grouping in the builder's insert menu | ✅ done (code). **No migration — `0003` built `pattern_categories` and seeded five rows, and `createPattern` has accepted a `description` since Phase 4.** Both were written by nothing: the rail renders `pattern.description` and fell back to "N sections" for every pattern that has ever existed, and `category_id` referenced five rows no screen could reach |
| B | Admin — the Theme history screen | `listThemeHistory`/`listThemePublishEvents`/`rollbackTheme`, `/admin/theme/history`, `RevisionEntityType` | ✅ done (code). **No migration — `0017` put `theme` on `document_table()`'s allowlist, so `publish_document` has written a `revisions` and a `publish_events` row for every theme publish since.** The data existed and no screen read it: the one change with the widest blast radius in the admin — the root layout's `<style>`, which reaches all 65 static pages — was the only one an editor could neither review nor undo |
| A | `mg.accentSerif` compiled to nothing | `--mg-accent-serif-rgb` in all three contexts, `CHANNEL_TWIN` in `lib/domain/theme.ts`, the palette entry in channel form | ✅ done (code), **and it found something much larger — see Known issues.** Tailwind drops an alpha utility whose colour is a bare `var()`, so **all fifteen** `mg-accentSerif/NN` classes emitted no CSS: every admin error border and tint, the builder's invalid-block frame, the danger Button and Badge, and the sign-in and forgot-password error boxes. Verified in the built stylesheet, not deduced. ⚠️ **`fg`, `bd` and `bg` are broken the same way, across 417 usages in 129 files, and are deliberately NOT fixed** — that one is the brand's |
| B | Admin — `media_assets` dimensions | `lib/domain/imageDimensions.ts` (PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP header parser, pure), wired into `uploadAsset` | ✅ done (code). **No migration — `0002` built `width`/`height` and `insertAsset` has accepted them since Phase 5a.** No caller ever passed one, so every asset held null and `AssetDetails`' "Dimensions" row — guarded on `asset.width && asset.height` — has never rendered for anything. A header parser rather than a dependency: no decode, no package, and pure enough to live in `lib/domain`. 15 tests against **real encoded images**, all three WebP containers included |
| B | Admin — the publish dialog's wording | `DOCUMENT_NOUN` and `publicPathForDocument` in `lib/domain/routes.ts` | ✅ done (code). **No migration.** The shared builder's publish dialog said "the live version of the page" for all six document types and printed `/{slug}` for two that have no URL — publishing a pattern advertised a page at `/editorial-trio` that has never existed and cannot be made to |
| B | Products admin — the revalidation gap | `revalidatePublicProduct` in all five of `catalog-actions.ts` | ✅ done (code). **A live bug found while building the picker, not a consequence of it.** All five actions revalidated only `/admin/products/{id}`. For variants that was invisible — they had no public surface — but **the gallery has fed the public PDP since Phase 7b**, so an editor who added a photograph saw the admin agree with them and the PDP keep the old one for up to an hour. Exactly the failure `revalidate.ts` was extracted to prevent, in the one file that never called it |
| B | Hardening — the public write endpoint | `0026`'s `rate_limits` + `rate_limit_hit()`, `lib/services/rateLimit.ts`, two buckets on `POST /api/newsletter`, a `throttled` state in the shared sign-up hook | ✅ done (code) and **probed against the built route**: ten 201s from one forwarded address, then 429 with `Retry-After: 600`, and a different address still 201. **In Postgres rather than in the process** — Railway can run more than one container, so a `Map` keyed by IP enforces a limit that varies with scheduling and reads as working in every test. ⚠️ **The per-caller bucket can be defeated by rotating `x-forwarded-for` and there is no fix for that** — unlike `x-forwarded-host`, an IP cannot be validated against anything we already know — which is why there is a **global** bucket as well. Probe rows and counters deleted afterwards and the table confirmed empty |
| B | Hardening — the current-password gate | `app/auth/_lib/recovery.ts`, the marker set at `/auth/callback`, the conditional field and check on `/admin/password` | ✅ done (code); 10 unit tests on the form, 1 new E2E. Closes the Known-issues bullet that asked for GoTrue's reauthentication nonce — **which it does not use, and the bullet's second clause is why**: what the gate needs is to tell a recovery session from any other, not a second email. ⚠️ The E2E submits the account's **existing** password as the new one, so a broken gate fails an assertion rather than locking the suite out of its own account |
| B | Ingestion — the Shopify 429 | `RetryPolicy` in `http.ts`, `retryAfterMs`, `RATE_LIMIT_RETRY` in the adapter, a `wait` seam on `AdapterContext` | ✅ done (code); 9 new unit tests, all with time injected so proving a two-second wait costs none. **Reverses a recorded decision** — see the decisions log for why the old reasoning was right about the danger and wrong about the magnitude |
| B | Media — a search index nothing could reach | `search_vector` (stored generated) + its GIN index in `0026`; `tsQuery` and the fts-then-`ilike` path in `listAssets` | ✅ done (code); 5 new unit tests. `0002`'s index was over an **expression**, which PostgREST cannot filter on, so the library has scanned four columns with a leading-wildcard `ilike` for four phases while paying write cost on an index nothing read. ⚠️ **Full-text alone would have been a downgrade, measured on the live library**: a filename is one token, so `to_tsvector('english','hero-cover.jpg')` is `'hero-cover.jpg':1` — `hero:*` matches and **`cover:*` matches nothing**. Hence the substring fallback, run only when full-text matches zero |
| B | `products.status` promised a behaviour nothing implements | the narrowed CHECK in `0026` | ✅ done. `0005` allowed `'scheduled'`; `products` has no `scheduled_for`, is absent from `SCHEDULABLE_TYPES` and from `schedulable_document_table()`. Unreachable from the admin, which is exactly why removing it beats leaving it — the next person to add a status dropdown gets a value that looks supported and a product that never publishes. Verified zero rows use it; the migration raises rather than corrupting if that is false elsewhere |

### ⚠️ Corrections to earlier guidance in this file

Instructions below that were true during Track A and are **now wrong**. A session acting on the old version will undo correct work.

- **"Cart + catalog are FINISHED — do not rebuild"** — no longer accurate. `lib/cart/CartProvider.tsx` now delegates its money maths to `lib/domain/pricing.ts`. This fixed a real bug: the original ran `Math.round()` over a **pounds** value, discounting a £145 subtotal by £22.00 instead of £21.75 — overcharging members 25p and able to flip the £50 free-shipping threshold. Do not revert it. The **rules** (15%, ≥£50, qty-0-removes) are unchanged and still correct.
- **Money is integer pence**, not pounds. `lib/domain/money.ts` converts at the boundary. The DB stores `price_pence integer`.
- **`lib/queries.ts` and `lib/supabase/` no longer exist.** Use `lib/db/{client,server,admin,public}.ts`. Repositories replace `queries.ts` in Phase 2, and `public.ts` arrived with Phase 7a — it is the cookie-free client the public site reads through, and using `server.ts` there instead silently forfeits static rendering.
- **`supabase/migrations/0001_init.sql` no longer exists.** It was never applied and is superseded by `0001`–`0009`.
- **`supabase/seed.sql` no longer exists either.** It was `0001_init.sql`'s partner — its own header said "after `0001_init.sql`" — and was missed when that migration was removed. It inserted `products.price` where the real schema has `price_pence`, so it aborted `supabase start` with `42703`, which meant **the integration and E2E jobs had never once run in CI**: both start a stack, and both died before reaching a test. Deleted. `scripts/seed.ts` is the maintained seeder, reads from the demo modules and is idempotent.
- **The Sanity scaffold is gone** (`sanity/`, `lib/sanity/`, `sanity.config.ts`, `@sanity/*`, `styled-components`). Do not reference it.
- **Stripe is out of scope** for now, by decision. Checkout stays demo-only. The commerce layer manages catalog/inventory/merchandising, not payments.
- **The app is no longer "100% demo data with no env vars."** It needs `.env.local` (see below) and reads a live Supabase project.

### ✅ Phase 7c is verified end to end — the live project is seeded

The editorial content is in the live project and **every check has now run and passed**:

- `tests/integration/publicEditorial.test.ts` — **green**. All 53 articles deep-equal the demo module field for field, and all five category pages resolve — through the real binding engine, against the real database, under real RLS — to exactly the props the route composed in code before this phase.
- `npm run build` — 53 article pages and 5 category pages prerendered from the database, all `● (SSG)` with a 1h revalidate.
- **CI is green on PR #19** — all three jobs, including the 29-spec E2E suite and the visual regression step, against a stack CI seeded from scratch with `scripts/seed.ts`. That is the independent confirmation the hand-carried SQL could not give.
- `npm run test:visual` — **all 16 public baselines pass**, including `category-style` and `article` in both themes: the first time either has rendered from Supabase, pixel-identical to the Track A screenshots.
- Rendered-markup spot checks: `/film` prints `12 MIN FILM`, `/culture` carries its own 042 lead with zero references to the unfiled cover story, every category renders exactly 7 article links, and an unknown category or article slug still returns **404**.

Live counts: 53 articles (35 filed across 5 categories), 10 authors, 28 tags, 30 `article_tags`, 159 `article_relations`, 5 category documents.

**Re-seeding is `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts`** from `design_handoff_modern_gentlemen/starter` — idempotent, and what CI runs. Note it overwrites `draft_data`/`published_data` for categories and products. ⚠️ **That stopped being harmless at Phase 7d** — a category is now an editable document, so re-seeding a project someone is editing discards their category layouts, exactly as it already did for navigation. Fine on a fresh database and in CI, which is what the script is for.

### What runs today

Public site: **every public route reads Supabase.** The homepage and store came in 7a/7b (`lib/services/publicContent.ts`, `publicCatalog.ts`); Phase 7c added `lib/services/publicEditorial.ts` for `/[category]` and `/article/[slug]`. All of them go through the cookie-free `lib/db/public.ts`, so the routes stay statically rendered with a 1h revalidate. Every module in `lib/demo/` is now a **seed source and test fixture**, with no exceptions left. Section props now pass through `lib/blocks/normalize.ts` on the way in — verified to leave the rendered markup of all 65 prerendered pages byte-identical.

New in Track B: **`/sign-in`** and a gated **`/admin`**. `middleware.ts` refreshes the session and redirects unauthenticated `/admin` traffic.

**The admin is now a real application** (Phase 4). `/admin` is the dashboard — recent edits plus the resolved permission set. `/admin/pages` lists, creates and deletes pages. **`/admin/pages/[id]` is the builder**: a dnd-kit canvas rendering the real section components live, a section library derived from the manifests, a properties panel with a control for every field kind, autosave, publish with validation, snapshots, preview links, and `/admin/pages/[id]/history` for revisions, publish events and rollback.

**Hovering or focusing a library entry previews the section**, rendered from the real component at desktop width and scaled down — not a thumbnail, so it cannot disagree with what an editor actually gets. One is rendered at a time.

**Sections nest, and the shape is row → column → anything.** A container declares a `slot` on its manifest, holds other blocks in `BlockNode.children`, and renders them through a `SectionRenderer` that recurses only for a manifest with a slot. **There are two containers**: `columns` is the row, and it accepts **only** `column` children (`allow: ["column"]`, `direction: "horizontal"`); a `column` accepts anything, including another row. A nested block is selectable, editable, lockable, duplicable and draggable exactly like a root one.

⚠️ **A `columns` row arrives holding two columns rather than empty** — `insertChildren` seeds them — and `column` is `hidden` from the insert menu, because a column outside a row renders but means nothing. **An empty *row* is publish-invalid by design** (`min: 1`); an empty **column** is deliberately legal, because that is how a row offsets its content or leaves a middle cell open.

**A section can now be dragged from the library onto the canvas**, and dropped at an exact position — including onto a page with nothing on it yet. The drop targets are explicit `gap:N` droppables that exist only while a library item is in flight, so block reordering is untouched. **Click-to-insert is unchanged and remains the accessible path**; the two coexist on one control only because of the drag's 6px activation constraint, which is now load-bearing rather than incidental (see the decisions log).

**Patterns come in two kinds now, and both mean what they say.** A **detachable** pattern copies its blocks into the page at insert time; a **synced** one inserts a single `patternRef` node holding a `_ref`, and the public paths substitute the pattern's published blocks when the page renders — so editing the pattern updates every page using it. The pattern's own `sync_mode` decides which happens, chosen once at creation. The canvas shows a reference as a card rather than as the pattern's content (preview is where the result is shown), and offers **Detach a copy** as the one-way door out. ⚠️ Only **published** patterns expand: the live site reads `published_data` and nothing else, and the card says so when a synced pattern has never been published.

**Every one of the six document types opens in the builder now**, and templates were the last. `/admin/templates` lists, creates, renames and deletes them; `/admin/templates/[id]` is the same builder every other type uses, with an **area switcher** above the canvas. A template's payload is `{ areas: { <name>: BlockNode[] } }` rather than one ordered list, so the store holds one area as its tree and keeps the rest in `doc.rest`; `treeKey` is a payload *path* (`areas.main`) rather than a key. Areas are editor-named — add, rename, remove — and a new template arrives holding one called `main`. ⚠️ **Nothing public renders a template yet**: `template_assignments` resolves one to a record and `resolveTemplateFor` has no caller on the render path. This is the editor, not the renderer, and previews are switched off for templates for the same reason.

**`/admin/integrations`** — product feeds. A source is created against `product_sources`, its fields are mapped onto ours in `feed_field_mappings`, and a run stages everything it proposes into `import_items` for review before anything reaches the catalogue. Nothing about a run is destructive: an imported product arrives as a **draft**, an update never rewrites the slug (it is the public URL) and never touches `draft_data` (that is the builder's). `/admin/integrations/jobs/[jobId]` is the review screen — per-item diffs, the feed's own record behind a Payload button, approve/reject, then apply.

**Every public image goes through Next's optimiser.** `components/ui/MediaImage.tsx` is the site's `<img>` — `next/image` with `fill` and a per-slot `sizes` from `IMAGE_SIZES` — and `components/ui/imageUrl.ts` covers the two places `next/image` cannot reach, a `<video poster>` and a CSS `background-image`, via Next's `getImageProps`. A src the optimiser would refuse (a third-party host an editor typed, an SVG) is rendered `unoptimized` rather than throwing, which on a public route would be a 500. The homepage's image weight is **300 KB on mobile, down from 3,936 KB**; `npm run test:perf` is what keeps it there. ⚠️ **Adding an image means picking a `slot`** — leaving `sizes` off makes `next/image` assume `100vw` and quietly undoes the lot.

**The app is split into route groups.** Public routes live in `app/(site)/`, the admin in `app/(admin)/`, and the root layout holds only `<html>`, fonts, the theme boot script and `ThemeProvider`. URLs are unchanged — route groups are not path segments. This is what closed the "`/admin` renders inside the public header and footer" issue.

Also **`/preview/[token]`** — renders any document's *draft* to whoever holds a valid, unexpired token, through the same `SectionRenderer` the live site uses. Verified end to end against the live project: the draft renders, an unknown token shows the expiry page rather than a 500, and the page is `noindex, nofollow, nocache`.

Admin account: `welcome@moderngentlemen.co`, role `admin` (**44** permissions — this line said 40 until `0021` added the `category.*` four, and `tests/e2e/auth.spec.ts` had the same number hard-coded and went red for it; the spec now derives it from `PERMISSIONS.length`, and **so should anything else that needs it**). Created by `scripts/create-admin.ts` — idempotent, safe to re-run.

### Environment

`design_handoff_modern_gentlemen/starter/.env.local` (gitignored; never commit real values):
`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, `JOBS_SECRET`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`.

**Railway → Variables needs all five**, and this line said "the first three" until 2026-08-09 — which is how `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` went eight phases unset and `JOBS_SECRET` went four undeclared. What each is for, so a future addition is not guessed at:

| Variable | Needed by | Fails as |
|---|---|---|
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` · `..._ANON_KEY` | every public route, the build, middleware | the layout throws; nothing renders |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` | canonicals, sitemap, robots, JSON-LD | build dies on `/_not-found` naming nothing |
| `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` | the scheduled-publish job only | jobs route 500 (503 since `f0e971d`) |
| `JOBS_SECRET` | the jobs route's bearer check | jobs route 503, or 401 if it drifts from the Actions secret |

The repository *also* needs `JOBS_SECRET` as an Actions **secret** and `SITE_URL` as an Actions **variable** — different places, easily confused, and the workflow fails loudly if only one of the pair is set.

### Architecture & patterns to REUSE (don't reinvent)

**Track A (unchanged):**
- **Page content = an ordered `Block[]`** rendered by `components/SectionRenderer.tsx` via `components/sections/registry.ts`; one component per archetype, variants via a `variant` prop.
- **Shared primitives:** `components/ui/*` (`Button`, `Eyebrow`/`MonoLabel`, `clsx`) and `components/store/*` (`ProductCard`, `QtyStepper`, `OrderSummary`, `Field`/`SelectField`). **Reuse `Field` for admin forms** — the sign-in page does.
- **Tokens/layout:** `.container-mg` (1320px, site only — not in `/admin`), the `mg.*` Tailwind tokens, `[data-darkband]`, `.mg-underline`.
- **Chrome is built once** in `app/layout.tsx`.

**Track B (new):**
- **`lib/blocks/`** — one `defineBlock()` manifest per section block, and the only answer to "what fields does this block have, and what is a valid value?". A **pure leaf**: no component references, no I/O (ESLint enforces it). Field descriptors *derive* the Zod schema — never hand-write a second one. `normalize.ts` is the forgiving render path, `validate.ts` the strict publish path, `binding.ts` the dynamic-content contract with injected sources, `expand.ts` pattern `_ref` expansion, `diff.ts` version comparison. **Adding a block means adding a manifest**; `conformance.test.ts` fails the build otherwise.
- **Publishing is a database transaction, not application code.** `publish` / `unpublish` / `snapshot` / `rollback` / `schedule` / `autosave` are `security invoker` SQL functions (`0010_publishing.sql`, `0011_autosave.sql`), called via `supabase.rpc()`. They write the payload, the revision and the audit event together or not at all. **Never reimplement any of this as a sequence of PostgREST calls** — the autosave bug below is what that costs.
- **Every operation that writes a revision advances `version`** — publish, snapshot, restore *and* autosave. `revisions` is unique on `(entity_type, entity_id, version)`, so anything writing history without advancing the counter collides with whatever already claimed that number. Do not add a sixth operation that "just records" without taking a version.
- **`lib/db/repositories/`** — the polymorphic document repository plus per-entity helpers. Every function takes the client as its first argument, so the caller decides whether RLS applies. `app/**` and `components/**` are **barred by ESLint** from importing these directly; go through `lib/services`.
- **`lib/domain/`** — pure: types, Zod schemas, business rules. No I/O, no React, no framework. `money.ts`, `pricing.ts`, `permissions.ts` live here.
- **`lib/db/`** — `client.ts` (browser), `server.ts` (caller's session + RLS — use this for admin writes), `admin.ts` (service-role; scripts/jobs/tests only), `database.types.ts` (generated, `npm run db:types`).
- **`lib/services/`** — orchestration + `requirePermission()`. `auth.ts` exposes `getCurrentUser`/`requireUser`/`requirePermission`, cached per render.
- **Layering is enforced by ESLint** (`no-restricted-imports` in `.eslintrc.json`): `app → services → db`; `domain`, `blocks`, `render`, `integrations` are leaves. If the rule complains, the design is wrong.
- **Authorisation is three-layered:** RLS policies (deepest, unbypassable) → `requirePermission()` in services → permission-filtered UI. Permissions are `resource.action` rows in the DB, not an enum.
- **Versioning convention** on every editable entity: `draft_data` / `published_data` / `version` / `status`, with a single polymorphic `revisions` table.
- **`components/admin/`** — the admin application. `ui/*` is its design system (built because the site's own primitives structurally could not serve — see the decisions log); `builder/*` is the canvas, store and panels; `fields/*` is the field→control mapping. That mapping lives here and not in `lib/blocks` because `lib/blocks` is a pure leaf and cannot import React.
- **The builder store is a factory, not a singleton** (`components/admin/builder/store.ts`). zustand + immer, one store per document, held in React context. Undo is a stack of previous tree *references* — cheap only because immer shares structure and `mapBlocks` never mutates. **Never mutate a tree outside `produce`.** zustand v5 dropped the default shallow comparison, so every selector must be atomic or wrapped in `useShallow`, or it re-renders forever.
- **The builder reaches persistence only through server actions passed in as props.** A server action reference is serializable across the server→client boundary, so `components/admin/builder/` imports nothing from `app/`, the ESLint layering holds trivially, and the store is testable with plain fakes. Same shape as `binding.ts`'s injected sources.
- **Admin server actions return `ActionResult`, never throw.** An exception crossing the boundary reaches the client as a digest with no message. `app/(admin)/admin/_lib/` holds the type and the error translation; it is underscore-prefixed so Next never routes it, and the type lives outside the `"use server"` files because those may export only async functions.

### Setup/deploy gotchas ALREADY FIXED (don't re-hit)

- `package-lock.json` is committed (Railway's `npm ci` needs it).
- `starter/railway.json` buildCommand is **`npm run build`** only — Nixpacks already runs `npm ci`; a second one hits `EBUSY`.
- Railway **Root Directory must stay `design_handoff_modern_gentlemen/starter`**.
- **Playwright is pinned to 1.56.1** to match the browsers preinstalled at `/opt/pw-browsers` (build 1194). Never run `playwright install` locally; CI installs its own.
- **The Supabase CLI is pinned too**, via `SUPABASE_CLI_VERSION` at the top of `ci.yml`. `supabase/setup-cli@v1` with `version: latest` resolves through the GitHub API on every run, and it **rate-limited on 2026-08-09** — `Failed to resolve latest Supabase CLI release: rate limit exceeded` killed the Integration job 21 seconds in, on a commit that touched only `PROGRESS.md`. Two jobs start a stack, so `latest` cost two lookups per run *and* left the toolchain irreproducible: the same migrations would meet a different binary next month. Top-level, for the reason `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` is — a third stack-starting job added later would otherwise quietly reintroduce `latest`. Bumping it is a deliberate act; the pinned value is what `latest` resolved to at the time, so adopting it changed nothing. (`package.json`'s `db:types` still calls `npx --yes supabase`, which resolves latest the same way — developer-invoked rather than CI, so a smaller problem, but the same shape.)
- **`@supabase/ssr` must stay ≥ 0.12.** Version 0.5.x cannot resolve the generated `Database` generic — every query silently degrades to `never`, discarding type safety across the data layer.
- **Route files may only export the page component** and Next's reserved symbols. Exporting a data array from `app/page.tsx` breaks `next build`; that is why `DEMO_SECTIONS` lives in `lib/demo/home-sections.ts`.
- **`server-only` is not used in `lib/db/admin.ts`** — it throws when imported outside Next, which breaks the seed script and test fixtures. A runtime browser check guards it instead.
- **The `PROGRESS.md freshness` CI step did nothing at all until 2026-08-09**, on every PR since it was written — and it is the third "a check nobody watches is a check that does nothing" found in one session. `actions/checkout` clones shallow, so `origin/<base>` is not a ref on the runner and the diff died with `fatal: ambiguous argument 'origin/main...HEAD': unknown revision`; the step's `|| true` swallowed that, `CHANGED` came out empty, both greps matched nothing, and it exited 0 having checked nothing. **It was found by reading a green log** — which is the only way a silent no-op is ever found, and the reason the env checker above was verified by making it fail first. Now: an explicit `git fetch --no-tags --depth=1 origin <base>` (one branch, shallow — no other job needs full history and this repo carries a 15 MB screenshot bundle), a **two-dot** `FETCH_HEAD..HEAD` diff (on a `pull_request` event HEAD is already the merge, so two-dot is exactly the PR's changes and three-dot would want a merge base a shallow fetch may lack), and **no `|| true`** — a failed fetch now emits a `::warning::` and exits 0, so the step still cannot block a hotfix but can no longer fail invisibly.
- ⚠️ **Prettier does not check `PROGRESS.md` or `design_handoff_modern_gentlemen/CLAUDE.md`, so "format:check clean" is not evidence for a change to either.** `ci.yml` runs the Prettier step with `working-directory: design_handoff_modern_gentlemen/starter`, and `prettier --check .` walks only that directory — both of these files sit above it. Pointed at them explicitly, Prettier reports style issues in **both**: they have never been formatted, so `--write` would reflow each in its entirety and bury whatever the change actually was. **Leave them unformatted — that is the consistent state, not an oversight.**
  - Asserted the other way round in PR #30's body ("Prettier covers `PROGRESS.md`") before anyone checked, and repeated as a verification step in two plans. Fourth instance of the shape above, with a twist: **the check is real, green, and simply never looked at the file** — which is harder to notice than a check that silently no-ops, because the green tick is honest about something else.
  - What *does* watch this file is the `PROGRESS.md freshness` step, and it checks that the file **changed** — not that it is well-formed, and certainly not that it is true. Nothing automated can do the latter; that is what the corrections habit here is for.
- **`getByRole("alert")` is never unique in a Playwright spec here — and this file already said so.** Next mounts `<div role="alert" aria-live="assertive" id="__next-route-announcer__">` into every page, so an assertion on that role matches the announcer as well as whatever the page renders. Match the element too (`p[role="alert"]`), since the announcer is always a `div`; the admin's own `Toast` is deliberately `role="status"` and does not compete. ⚠️ **The genuinely useful part is that it cost two red CI runs on the theme PR anyway, after the products phase had already recorded it** — see the PR #11 entry in the Phase 6a log below, which even ends "worth remembering the next time an accessible-role locator looks obviously specific." The second red run followed a grep that "confirmed" the role was unused: a grep of **`app/` and `components/`**, which cannot see a framework-injected element. `PROGRESS.md` could, and was not searched. **Grep the handoff before concluding a trap is novel** — this document is the first place to look, not the place to write the lesson down afterwards.
- **`click()` then `page.reload()` is a race, and this repo turns it into data loss.** A button that fires a server action returns immediately; reloading navigates away and can abort the request in flight. `integrations.spec.ts` did exactly that after "Save mappings" and **went red on a docs-only PR with byte-identical application code** (PR #29), having passed on PR #28 ten minutes earlier. What made the symptom `element(s) not found` rather than a stale value is the second half: **`replaceMappings` is delete-then-insert and not transactional**, so an aborted save can land the delete and lose the insert, leaving the source with *zero* mappings — and every retry re-ran the same race. Fix: wait for the app's own toast (`await expect(page.getByText("Mappings saved")).toBeVisible()`), which is pushed only after the action returns.
  - ⚠️ **The genuinely uncomfortable part: this was already the convention and the newest spec broke it.** `theme.spec.ts`, `products.spec.ts` and `articles.spec.ts` all had `click()` → await the toast → `reload()` before the integrations screen was written. It was not a novel trap; it was a pattern three specs already followed. **So "grep the handoff before concluding a trap is novel" is not enough — grep the neighbouring specs too.** The rule this file keeps rediscovering is that the codebase usually already knows.
  - Match the toast by **text**, not `getByRole("status")`: the toast container is always mounted even when empty. It is deliberately `status` and not `alert`, so it does not collide with Next's route announcer.
- ⚠️ **Redirects in route handlers must use `app/auth/_lib/publicUrl.ts`, and neither `request.url` *nor* `request.nextUrl` is safe on its own.** This bullet used to say "build them from `request.nextUrl.clone()`, not `new URL(..., request.url)`" and that was **wrong in the deployment it was written for**: behind Railway's proxy a route handler sees the container's address in both, and the live `/auth/callback` was serving `Location: https://localhost:8080/...`. The rule that survives is the *reason* — auth cookies are host-scoped, so a redirect across hosts strands the user. The mechanism is `x-forwarded-host`, honoured only when it matches `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`'s host so it cannot be forged into an open redirect.
  - **Middleware is exempt**: its `nextUrl` does reflect the original request. That asymmetry is exactly why this survived — the sign-in gate redirected correctly the whole time, which made the route handlers look fine by association.
  - **It is invisible to every check in this repo.** Tests, build, E2E and the baselines all run single-host with no proxy. `curl -o /dev/null -w '%{redirect_url}'` against the live URL found it in one request; do that after touching a redirect.

- ⚠️ **`grep srcset` on the served HTML finds nothing, and that is not a regression.** React serves the attribute as **`srcSet`**, camelCase, in both the markup and the RSC flight payload — so a case-sensitive grep against the live site reports zero and looks exactly like the image optimiser having silently stopped working. Found by doing it: `curl … | grep -c 'srcset'` returned `0` on a deploy that was demonstrably serving seven of them. Use `grep -i`, or check for `_next/image` instead, which is the thing that actually matters. Costs a minute, and the minute is spent believing a shipped feature is broken.

- ⚠️ **A unit test that compares a `path.relative()` result to a literal is Linux-only, and CI cannot tell you.** `lib/db/apiKeyCoverage.test.ts` walks the source for Supabase client constructions and asserts the list contains `"lib/db/admin.ts"` and four more. `relative()` returns the **host's** separator, so on a Windows runtime the walk yields `lib\db\admin.ts`, the literal misses, and one test fails out of 1,359 — with nothing wrong in either the test's subject or its logic. Found by someone running the suite in a non-Linux environment while evaluating the repo in another agent tool; every gate here and in CI was green, and always would be, because both are Linux. Fixed with a `repoPath()` helper that splits on `sep` and rejoins on `/`. **The fix cannot be verified on this machine** — on Linux `sep` is already `/`, so the change is a provable no-op locally and only the reporting environment can confirm it. That is the honest state: correct by construction, unverifiable by the suite that carries it. The two other tests importing `node:path` (`alphaUtilities`, `theme`) only `join()` paths to read files, which is separator-agnostic; `scripts/check-env.mjs` calls `relative()` but only to print, so neither is affected.
  - The same session found the repo's `npm` scripts unrunnable in that environment for an unrelated reason (no `npm` on the runtime). That is not a repo problem — every script is a thin wrapper over a binary in `node_modules/.bin`, so `./node_modules/.bin/tsc --noEmit` and `./node_modules/.bin/vitest run --project=unit` are exact substitutes. Worth knowing before concluding the project does not build somewhere.

### Next phase (recommended order)

> ⚠️ **Read this sub-section, not the numbered list below it.** The list is a history of slices that are now *done* — it is kept because each entry records what a slice cost against what it was predicted to cost, which is the only reason any of it is still worth reading. **What is actually outstanding is here.**

**Nothing is half-built and nothing is in flight.** Every slice is merged; if that ever stops being true, an in-flight bullet at the top of this file says so — check there rather than trusting this sentence, which is written for the steady state. There is no partially-landed feature to finish, so the next session picks a slice rather than resuming one.

**The candidates, in the order they are worth doing:**

**Every Track B slice is done, so is Track A's Phase 7, so is every build item, and the engineering backlog under Known issues is now empty of anything a session may start on its own.** The two correctness defects the variant picker exposed shipped; **two of the three brand decisions were taken by the owner and implemented** (contrast, and the categories delete); and the four hardening slices that were sitting in Known issues — the newsletter rate limit, the current-password gate, the Shopify 429 backoff, and the media search index with `products.status` beside it — have all landed. See the decisions log: **three of the four reversed a paragraph in this file, and none of the reversals needed information that was unavailable when the original decision was taken.**

**What is left is not code.** Item 5 (newsletter consent and double opt-in) is a legal statement the brand owns; item 6 (the canonical domain) the owner is handling; and the launch gates in the next section — the live card input, the member discount that is client-side only, and the hot-linked video — are product decisions with money and rights attached. **So the next session still has nothing it may start without asking.** ⚠️ Two engineering items remain genuinely open and are *not* free: the `draft_data` column a role-less authenticated account can still read (41 read sites across seven document tables; it needs a `security definer` read path, and it changes how every admin screen reads), and GoTrue's reauthentication nonce, which is the stronger version of the password gate that just shipped.

### Build work

1. ~~**Track A's Lighthouse / performance budget**~~ — **done.** Image sizing shipped and is now guarded by `npm run test:perf`; font loading needed nothing (`next/font` already self-hosts, preloads and supplies metric-adjusted fallbacks) and CLS was **already 0 on every route**, so the pass reduced to images, which were 83% of page weight. See the decisions log.

   ⚠️ **The prediction this file made about it was wrong in a useful way, and the mistake is the reusable part.** This item said a performance pass "changes image formats and font loading" and therefore "invalidates the sixteen baselines by construction", and advised bundling it with the contrast decision to move pixels once instead of twice. **It did not invalidate a single baseline** — all 16 passed untouched, including the WebP re-encodes, because `maxDiffPixelRatio: 0.01` sits above the per-pixel noise a re-encode adds. So the two were separable after all, and **the contrast decision is still open and still the brand's** (item 3). The lesson: *"this will move pixels"* is a measurement, not a deduction — and it cost nothing to check first, where bundling would have spent a brand decision to buy a re-record that was never needed.

2. ~~**The PDP variant picker**~~ — **done.** `product_variants` has a public surface at last: a picker on the PDP, the selected variant's price, sold-out options disabled, and the variant carried through the cart as its own line. **No migration** — `0005` built the table and `0019` already scoped its reads to a published parent, both checked rather than assumed. Baseline-safe because no seeded product has a variant row, which is asserted rather than hoped for.

   ⚠️ ~~**It also made two pre-existing things visible, and neither is fixed**~~ — **both are done**, and the reason they were deferred turned out not to survive measurement. They were held back because each "changes rendered output on a baseline-verified page", which was true of the member price and **false of the JSON-LD** (structured data is not pixels), and in the one case where it was true the change came in **under the tolerance the baselines already run at**. See the decisions log: *"this will move pixels" is a measurement* — recorded here for the second time in three slices, having been re-derived rather than read.

   ~~**The remaining smaller gaps**~~ — **all five are done.** `patterns` collects a description and a category (and the builder's insert menu groups by it); the publish dialog is worded per document type and no longer prints a URL for things that have none; `/admin/theme/history` exists and can roll back; `media_assets` dimensions are read from the file's own header on upload; and `mg.accentSerif`'s alpha utilities compile.

2a. ~~**The alpha-utility ramp**~~ — **done, and it was the largest thing in the backlog for a reason nobody had costed.** All four of `bg`, `fg`, `surface` and `bd` carry `--mg-*-rgb` twins now, so their ~413 alpha utilities emit CSS. `muted` and `faint` stay bare `var()` **permanently**: they are `rgba()` in dark, a twin can only come from a hex, and nothing uses them with an alpha — asserted rather than assumed, so writing one fails a test.

   ⚠️ **The entry described the work correctly and missed its consequence entirely, which is the part worth keeping.** It said repairing the tokens "lightens muted text and borders site-wide in one commit and needs all sixteen baselines re-recorded". Both true. What it did not say is that **the bug was hiding contrast failures**: 177 call sites were painting at full strength, which passes AA by accident, so making the site render its own design took the failing-node count from **6 to 110**. The repair and the opacity re-tune are one change, not two. **A bug can flatter an audit** — for three phases the a11y suite was measuring a site that was not rendering what the design specified.

### Decisions the brand owns, not a session

Each is held as a `KNOWN GAP` characterisation test that asserts today's behaviour and carries the instruction to invert it — so the suite goes red the day one is settled, which is the technique this file records working four times now.

3. ~~**The brand red at small sizes**~~ and ~~**the light grey ramp**~~ — **both done. Contrast is at zero across every public route in both themes**, with no exceptions list and no disabled rules, and the `KNOWN GAP` test that watched it has been deleted per its own retirement condition.

   ⚠️ **This entry was wrong three times, and each mistake generalises.** (1) "No single **red** clears 4.5:1 in both themes" — true but not about red: against `#0d0d0d` AA sets a luminance floor of 0.193 and against `#f4f4f4` a ceiling of 0.162, windows that do not overlap **for any hue**. (2) It framed the fix as "a theme-dependent accent", which was implemented literally and **measured to make things worse — 34 nodes became 46** — because `--mg-accent` fills the red CTA band as well as painting text; ink and fill want opposite luminances, so they are two tokens. (3) It said the light ramp must collapse into one step because "both need `#707070`" — that assumed moving each to the *minimum* passing value, when anything **at or below** `#707070` passes, so `#5a5a5a`/`#707070` keeps both steps. **Read the constraint as a range, not a point.**

4. ~~**`categories` delete permissions**~~ — **decided and done as `0023`.** Both routes require `category.delete`; `author` keeps create and rename and loses delete, which is what every other content type already says about that role. ⚠️ **The entry's premise was inherited from `0022` and was wrong in the table's favour**: `categories` has no permissive delete policy at all, so `category.delete` was not "additive" here — it appeared in the RLS catalogue **nowhere**, a permission the application checks and the database had never heard of. **Read `pg_policies`, not the previous migration's description of it.**

5. **Newsletter consent and double opt-in.** ⚠️ **New, and it is the brand's rather than a session's.** Addresses are captured since `0024` but land as `pending`, and nothing writes `confirmed`, because nothing confirms them. This is a UK brand ("Free shipping on UK orders over £50"), so UK GDPR/PECR wants a recorded lawful basis, consent wording beside the button, and a privacy notice to link to. Deciding it turns the success copy from "we've got your address" into "check your inbox" and unlocks the ESP slice behind it. **Do not let a session invent the consent wording** — that is a legal statement, not microcopy.

6. **The canonical domain.** Canonicals point at the Railway subdomain **by decision**. Switching is one environment variable plus redirects for whatever was indexed meanwhile — cheap now, less cheap after more of the site is indexed.

### Not code at all, and the real gate on launch

Recorded here because "the platform is nearly done" and "the product is nearly done" are different sentences, and this file has only ever tracked the first. **Stripe/checkout is out of scope by decision** and checkout is demo-only; membership is a flag rather than a subscription; **the newsletter captures to Supabase since `0024`, is rate-limited since `0026`, and reaches no ESP**; **every image and video is a placeholder with no rights cleared**. No amount of building finishes these.

⚠️ **Every clause in that sentence has now been audited against the code, and two of the three were understated.** Recorded because the newsletter one (below) proved that a plausible line in this paragraph is what stops anyone checking.

- **Checkout — accurate, and honest to the visitor.** `placeOrder` mints a random id, clears the bag and renders a confirmation; nothing is transmitted anywhere. The card number, name and expiry go into ephemeral React state, are used only to render "Card ending 1234" on the review step, and are **never sent, never stored**. The card fields deliberately carry no `autoComplete` (unlike the address fields, which do), so browsers neither autofill a saved card nor offer to save one. ⚠️ One line *was* false and is fixed here: the confirmation said "A confirmation is on its way to {email}" and nothing sends an email. It now describes what actually happened. **Residual, and it is a launch gate rather than a bug:** a live public page asks for a real card number. Nothing leaks it, but that input should not exist by the time real money can be taken.
- **Membership — accurate but understated in a way that constrains the Stripe slice.** It is not merely "a flag rather than a subscription": it is a **client-side `localStorage` flag** (`mg-member`), set by `cart.setMember(true)` with **no server call anywhere**. So the 15% member discount is self-service — any visitor can grant it from devtools. Impact today is zero because checkout charges nothing. ⚠️ **The moment Stripe lands, the discount must be recomputed server-side and the client flag treated as a hint, never as an authorisation.**
- **Imagery and video — accurate but seriously understated.** "Placeholders with no rights cleared" does not convey that **the live homepage hot-links a commercial film trailer from a third-party CDN**: `media.gamereactor.dk` (an F1 / Apple TV official trailer) plus three `upload.wikimedia.org` clips, confirmed in `pages.published_data` on the live project. That is three problems at once — a rights exposure, bandwidth taken from someone else's CDN on a URL that can break or be swapped without notice, and third-party requests from every homepage view. `lib/media.ts` documents all of this honestly at the source; **this paragraph did not, and this paragraph is what gets read.**

⚠️ **That newsletter clause was false until this session, and the way it was false is the lesson.** It read "the newsletter captures to Supabase and reaches no ESP" for several phases. There was **no table, no route handler, no repository and no service** — checked, not assumed. Both sign-up bands discarded the address on submit (`CtaBand`'s input was not even controlled) and then rendered "Thanks — you're on the list." or "SUBSCRIBED ✓" on **seven live pages**. So the site told every visitor they had subscribed to something with no record of them anywhere.

**The backlog line is what stopped anyone looking.** "Captures to Supabase, reaches no ESP" reads as a known, benign, half-finished integration — the kind of item you skip past — rather than as a false statement shown to the public. A wrong entry in a status document is worse than a missing one: a gap invites investigation and a plausible lie forecloses it. **When writing one of these, name what you checked, not what you assume is there.**

**Deliberately not next, and why** — so nobody re-derives these: **row support** (the builder has it, as `columns`; see the decisions log), **`template` sync modes**, and **`visibility.devices` at render** (honouring it needs a session in the site layout, which costs the whole public site its static rendering).

It closed that for an *assigned* template — and an editor cannot assign one without SQL, so from the admin the observable behaviour is unchanged.
