- [ ] ⚠️ **The E2E suite has a resource-starvation failure mode, and it looks exactly like a real bug.** On PR #39's first run, `media.spec.ts:155` and `products.spec.ts:67` both failed on a 30s timeout waiting for `/admin/products`'s "New product" button, through all three attempts; a storage-upload test in the same run failed twice and passed on the third. Re-running the same commit passed everything. **The tell is the clock, not the retries**: the failing run's E2E step took **5.2 minutes** and the passing one took **~2 minutes** for the same 59 specs, so a 2.5× slowdown pushed real journeys past a per-test timeout that is normally comfortable. Worth knowing because this repo has been bitten the other way too — PR #29 went red on "obviously flaky" E2E and it was a genuine non-transactional-save bug — so **neither "it's flake" nor "it's real" is the safe default.** The cheap discriminator is to compare the step duration against a known-good run before reading the failures. If this recurs, the fix is a longer per-test timeout or a less loaded runner, not a code change.
- [x] ~~**A pattern cannot be renamed after it is created.**~~ — **done.** `renameDocument` is generic over `DOCUMENT_TABLES`, `renamePage` delegates to it, and the patterns list has a Rename dialog. The original note follows, because the constraint it names still binds: `renamePage` (`lib/services/documents.ts`) is the only rename in the repo and it is page-specific — it writes `pages` through `renamePageRow`, and there is no generic equivalent. So a pattern's `name` and `key` are fixed at creation, and getting one wrong means delete and recreate. The generic path exists for everything else (`saveDraft`, `setStatus`, `deleteDocument` all take a `DocumentType`), so the fix is a `renameDocument` shaped like those, using `DOCUMENT_TABLES[type].titleColumn`/`slugColumn` — which is also what would let `renamePage` stop being a special case. ⚠️ Note the constraint it must keep: **an import never writes `slug` on an update**, because the slug is the PDP's URL; a rename UI for products would have to respect that.
- [x] ~~**Half of `patterns` is unreachable from the admin.**~~ — **closed**: `description` and `category_id` are collected by the create and edit dialogs and the builder's insert menu groups by category, and `sync_mode` is offered at creation. `preview_asset_id` is still written by nothing, which is the one part of this bullet that survives. Original text follows.
  **Half of `patterns` is unreachable from the admin.** `description` is never collected by the create form (so the library rail always falls back to "N sections"), and `category_id`, `preview_asset_id` and `sync_mode` have no UI at all. `pattern_categories` is a table with nothing writing to it, which is why the rail has one flat Patterns group rather than the grouped one the schema anticipated. `sync_mode` is deliberate — see the synced-pattern issue above — but the other three are simply not built yet.
- [x] ~~**The builder's publish dialog is worded for pages.**~~ — **closed** by `DOCUMENT_NOUN` and `publicPathForDocument` in `lib/domain/routes.ts`: the dialog is worded per document type and prints no URL for the two types that have none. Original text follows.
  **The builder's publish dialog is worded for pages.** It says "This makes the current draft the live version of the page" and prints the slug as `/{slug}`, which for a pattern renders `/editorial-trio` — a path that does not exist, since a pattern's key is not a URL. Cosmetic and shared: `PublishBar` is one component serving every document type, so fixing it means either per-type copy or wording that is true for all of them. Noted rather than patched, because the history-link defect in the same component *was* a real 404 and this is not.
- [x] ~~**`site_settings` is `using (true)`** and empty.~~ — **closed by `0025`**: reads are `is_staff()`. It was still empty and read by nothing when it was fixed, which is why it cost nothing. ⚠️ **If a genuinely public setting arrives, add a narrow policy naming those keys — do not restore `using (true)`.** Original text follows.
  **`site_settings` is `using (true)`** and empty. Whatever lands in it is world-readable the day it is written; a table named "settings" is the kind that eventually acquires an operational value nobody meant to publish.
- [x] ~~**A live session can change the account's password without knowing it.**~~ — **fixed, and the bullet named the shape of the fix correctly while naming the wrong mechanism.** It said this needed GoTrue's `reauthenticate()` nonce "applied only when the session did *not* arrive from a recovery link". The second clause is the whole design and the first is not required for it: what the gate needs is a way to tell a recovery session from any other, not a second email. `/auth/callback` is the only route that exchanges a one-time code, so a successful exchange there sets a short-lived `httpOnly` marker cookie (`app/auth/_lib/recovery.ts`), and `/admin/password` requires the current password whenever it is absent — verified against `createPublicClient()`, whose `persistSession: false` is what stops the check re-issuing the caller's own session mid-action. **The reauthentication nonce is still the stronger control and is still open**; what is closed is the four-keystroke takeover from an unattended browser. ⚠️ The E2E test for it submits the account's *existing* password as the new one, so a broken gate fails the assertion instead of locking the suite out of its own account.
- [ ] **Supabase's Redirect URLs allow list is deployment configuration nothing in the repo asserts.** `/forgot-password` sends `redirectTo` explicitly, and Supabase silently refuses any value not on that list — so recovery breaks in a way no test or build can catch, exactly like the `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`-in-Railway gap. `scripts/check-env.mjs` cannot see it either, since it is not an environment variable. The entry to add is `<site origin>/**`.
- [x] ~~**`replaceMappings` is delete-then-insert and not transactional**~~ — **closed by `0025`**: both statements are in one `security invoker` `plpgsql` function, so they roll back together. Proven by forcing the insert half to fail and asserting the previous set survives — the old path left zero. Original text follows.
  **`replaceMappings` is delete-then-insert and not transactional**, so an interrupted save — a closed tab, a dropped connection, a reload — can leave a source with **no mappings and no error**. Found via the E2E flake above, but it is an application issue rather than a test one: the test only made it visible. The fix is the shape `0010_publishing.sql` already established — a `security invoker` SQL function doing both statements in one transaction, called through `supabase.rpc()`. Needs a migration, so it wants its own slice; it was deliberately not bundled into a docs PR.
- [x] ~~**A Shopify run is not retried when Shopify rate-limits it.**~~ — **fixed, and the reasoning that deferred it was half right in a way worth keeping.** It said a silent retry "would turn one visibly failed run into a four-minute one". The danger is real; **the magnitude was assumed and is wrong by two orders**. Shopify's REST Admin API refills its leaky bucket at two requests a second and answers `Retry-After: 2.0` — it is asking for two seconds. Meanwhile a 429 on page seven of twelve threw away six successful pages, and the operator's only remedy was to run it again at the same page size into the same limit. `fetchCapped` now takes a `RetryPolicy`: 429 only, three attempts, a 5s ceiling on any single wait and a **10s ceiling on their sum** — the last is the one that actually bounds it, because without it "honour `Retry-After`" hands the provider control of how long a request is held open. `Retry-After` is parsed in both its forms and **anything unparseable falls back to a known delay rather than becoming `NaN`**, which would resolve instantly and turn the backoff into a hot loop against a server already refusing. **Still open, and genuinely separate: a run holds a server action open** — that is the queue, not the backoff.
- [x] ~~**Ingestion imports no images**~~ — **closed**: `images` is a `urlList` target and `applyJob` runs fetch → `uploadAsset` → `attachProductMedia`. ⚠️ That path is exercised nowhere and cannot honestly be — the ingestion E2E deliberately never runs an import — so everything decidable without a network was pushed into `lib/domain` and unit-tested instead. Original text follows.
  **Ingestion imports no images**, and Shopify widens this gap rather than narrowing it — a Shopify product's images are the thing a merchant most expects to arrive. `FEED_TARGET_FIELDS` has no image target, by decision: it needs binaries downloaded into Storage, catalogued in `media_assets` and reconciled against `product_media`, and it fails in ways a text mapping never does. A feed's image URLs are visible in the raw payload on the review screen, so nothing is lost — an imported product simply has no gallery until someone attaches one. This is the largest single gap between an imported product and a hand-entered one.
- [x] ~~**Ingestion runs are manual only.**~~ — **closed** by `SYNC_SCHEDULES`/`isSyncDue`, `lib/services/scheduledImports.ts`, `POST /api/jobs/run-imports` and the `scheduled-imports` workflow. A scheduled run still **stages** proposals and ends at `review`; applying is still a person's job, deliberately. Original text follows.
  **Ingestion runs are manual only.** `import_jobs.trigger` accepts `'scheduled'` and `'webhook'` and only `'manual'` has a caller; `product_sources.sync_schedule` is stored, editable by nobody, and read by nothing. Wiring a schedule means a second consumer of the jobs route from the scheduled-publish phase — read that phase's warning that `*/5` is nothing like every five minutes before relying on the timing.
- [ ] **A run holds a server action open for its whole duration**, because it fetches, maps and stages inline rather than queueing. Fine for the feed sizes this is built for (the adapter caps a response at 20 MB and times out at 30s by default) and wrong for a very large one. The staging insert is already chunked at 250 rows; the fetch and the per-item diff reads are what would need moving to the jobs route. `import_jobs.status` already has `'queued'` for the day that happens.
- [ ] ⚠️ **Every click is discarded for 50ms after a drop, in the product and not only in tests.** dnd-kit adds a capture-phase `click` listener on the document when a drag activates — the guard that stops a completed drag from *also* firing the library entry's click and inserting twice — and `detach()` removes it on a `setTimeout(…, 50)`. So a user who drops a section and clicks its toolbar inside that window gets nothing at all: no error, no visual hint, the handler simply never runs. It cost three flaky CI runs before the cause was found (see the decisions log). Left as-is because the guard is worth more than the window costs, but **anything built on the drag should know it exists** — a "drop, then immediately act on the block" flow would feel broken.
- [ ] **An empty `columns` row cannot be published**, by design: its slot declares `min: 1`, so a row dropped and left with no columns blocks publish. Recorded because it looks like a bug from the outside — do not "fix" it without deciding that a blank row should be allowed onto the live site. ⚠️ **An empty `column` is a different case and is allowed**: a blank cell is how a row offsets its content, so `column`'s slot declares no `min` at all.
- [x] ~~**A column cannot be reordered by dragging.**~~ — **done**, and it turned out to be two bugs rather than a missing feature. See the decisions log.
- [ ] **A block still cannot be dropped into a *non-empty* container.** Insertion strips are registered only during a *library* drag (`dragging` is computed from the library type), so moving an existing block has two routes: onto another block, or onto an empty container's placeholder. Dropping onto a block *inside* a container does move it in there, so the gap is narrower than it sounds — what is missing is choosing the position. Enabling strips for block drags is the fix, and it is safe now that a horizontal slot declares itself and refuses them.
- [ ] **A section preview mounts the real component on hover**, which for an image- or video-heavy block is real work on every hover. Acceptable today at the library's current size and one preview at a time; if the library grows or a section gets heavier, the cheap fix is a short hover delay before mounting rather than a thumbnail — a thumbnail reintroduces exactly the drift the preview exists to avoid.
- [x] ~~**`POST /api/newsletter` has no rate limiting**~~ — **fixed, and the bullet's own objection is what shaped the fix.** "An in-process counter is worthless on a platform that can run more than one container" was right, and the conclusion drawn from it — that this needed shared state "this deployment does not have" — was wrong: the deployment has Postgres. `0026` holds a fixed-window counter and a `security definer` `rate_limit_hit()`; `lib/services/rateLimit.ts` calls it. **Two buckets, and the second is the load-bearing one.** The per-caller bucket keys on `x-forwarded-for`, which is attacker-supplied and — unlike the `x-forwarded-host` case in `publicUrl.ts` — **cannot be validated**, because any IP is a plausible one. So a caller who rotates the header gets a fresh bucket every time, and what actually bounds the table is a **global** bucket no header can escape. ⚠️ **The limit is spent before the address is validated**, deliberately: validating first would make a malformed address a free request, which is the cheapest thing an abusive caller can send. That is why the per-caller limit is 10 and not 5 — a visitor correcting a typo spends budget doing it. Probed against the built route on a verified-free port: ten 201s, then 429 with `Retry-After`, and a different forwarded address still 201.

- [ ] `resolve_preview` still has no rate limiting (carried over from Phase 3). Tokens are 256-bit so brute force is impractical, but the endpoint is public and unthrottled. ⚠️ **The reason to revisit it is now cheaper than the reason to defer it**: `rate_limit_hit()` exists, `consumeRateLimit` is one call, and the only open question is what identity a preview link should key on.
- [ ] The builder has no keyboard shortcuts beyond `Cmd/Ctrl+S`. ~~and no drag-from-library~~ — **drag-from-library is done**, but it is **pointer-only by decision**: the library entries wire dnd-kit's pointer activator and deliberately *not* its keyboard one, because the `KeyboardSensor` claims Enter and Space and calls `preventDefault`, which would replace click-to-insert with a keyboard drag and leave no accessible path at all. Insert-at-the-selection by click is still that path. A keyboard *drag* would need its own affordance and its own announcements, and is unbuilt.
- [ ] `visibility.devices` is written but **not applied at render** — the control says so in its help text rather than pretending otherwise.
- [x] ~~`products.status` permits `'scheduled'` with no `scheduled_for` column~~ — **fixed in `0026`**: the CHECK is narrowed to `draft`/`published`/`archived`. This bullet framed it as "either dropping the value from a live CHECK or adding the column", and narrowing won — scheduling a *product* is a feature nobody has asked for, and adding the column would have been inventing it. Verified zero rows used it, and the migration raises rather than corrupting if that is ever false elsewhere.
- [x] ~~an asset used only in a product gallery is not "in use" as far as the delete refusal is concerned~~ — **fixed.** `media_usages` reconciliation still does not cover `product_media` (they are different tables with different lifecycles, and merging them would mean teaching reconciliation about a join no block tree mentions). Instead `deleteAsset` now asks **both**, via `productsForAsset` in the products repository, and `lib/domain/media.ts#galleryReferences` maps the result into the same `AssetUsage` shape so it flows through `AssetInUseError` and the admin's "Used in" panel unchanged. The panel therefore shows gallery references *before* an editor tries to delete, and `getAssetUsageViews` learned to link `product` — which also fixed a product's block-tree usage resolving a title and then offering nowhere to go.
  - **Why it mattered when it did.** The gap was harmless while the public store rendered from `lib/catalog.ts`: nothing downstream read the join. Phase 7b made `/shop` and every PDP read `product_media`, so the same unguarded delete cascades the gallery rows away and takes photographs off the live storefront. The bug did not change; its blast radius did. Worth remembering as a shape — a dormant defect that a *different* change arms.
- [x] ~~`0015_menu_item_read_scope.sql` is written and unapplied, and the live project has no menu rows~~ — **both applied by hand through the dashboard SQL editor, and verified.** Worth recording *why* it went that way: **every Supabase MCP call in that session returned "requires approval", including read-only ones**, and the refusal survived writing a local `permissions.allow` entry for both of the connector's tool-name prefixes — so it came from the connector gateway, not from Claude Code's permission layer. The connector's prefix also changed three times in one session (`mcp__Supabase__*` ↔ a UUID form), which makes any allowlist keyed to it perishable anyway. **The lesson for next time: when the MCP is refusing, do not spend the session negotiating with it — generate the SQL and use the dashboard.** The SQL was *generated from `lib/demo/navigation.ts`* rather than written by hand, so it could not drift from the fixture the integration test compares against, and emitted as pure 7-bit ASCII per the Phase 7c mojibake rule.
- [ ] A fresh container cannot run `npm run test:visual` or `npm run test:e2e` without a `.env.local` — `middleware.ts` needs `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`/`ANON_KEY` on every request or the Playwright web server never becomes ready. ⚠️ **This line used to end "placeholder values are enough for the visual suite", and that has been false since Phase 7a** — the site layout reads the database on every public route, so placeholders fail all 16 baselines. It is the same trap this file records setting twice elsewhere (line ~380 and "A new session's first actions"), left uncorrected here until the theme phase. **Real credentials are needed for the visual suite, the build and E2E**; E2E additionally needs an admin account.

## Still deferred
- [ ] Real membership subscriptions vs. flag-only
- [~] **Newsletter ESP integration.** ⚠️ The parenthetical here read "(Supabase capture works standalone)" and was false — nothing was captured at all until `0024`. **The capture layer is done now**: a table `anon` may INSERT and not read, `POST /api/newsletter`, and both bands wired to it with real error states. What remains is genuinely two things, in order: **consent + double opt-in** (a decision, not a task — UK GDPR/PECR wants a recorded lawful basis, wording beside the button, and a privacy notice to link to; rows are `pending` and nothing writes `confirmed` until it exists), then **the ESP adapter itself**, which should follow the `SourceAdapter` precedent in `lib/integrations/commerce/`.
- [ ] Production imagery/video + rights
- [ ] Remaining article templates & Section Library modules
